# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31

## A

1. Kenya is highly vulnerable to climate change patterns and related water scarcity due to its reliance on agriculture as a major economic activity. The country faces challenges such as drought, floods, and landslides leading to food insecurity, displacement of people, and loss of livelihoods. These changes are exacerbated by poor infrastructure and weak institutional capacity, limiting the ability to adapt to climate change.

2. The effects of climate change and related disasters have the potential to adversely impact many Kenyans given that 80% of the population depends on agriculture as their primary source of income. Climate change is also expected to affect water resources, which are critical for agricultural production and domestic use. This will have significant impacts on food security and economic development.

3. The project aims to strengthen the capacity of local communities to adapt to climate change and improve their resilience to natural disasters. It seeks to enhance community-based adaptation strategies that build on existing knowledge and practices. The project will also support the development of early warning systems for extreme weather events, such as floods, droughts, and landslides.

4. The project will be implemented in the Upper Tana River Catchment Area, which is one of the most vulnerable regions in Kenya to climate change. The area is characterized by high levels of poverty, low levels of education, and limited access to basic services. The project will work with local communities to develop and implement adaptation strategies that are tailored to their specific needs.

5. The project will directly benefit 1,196,237 people in the project area. It will also contribute to the achievement of the national climate change goals and targets set out in the National Climate Change Action Plan (NCCAP) 2018-2022. The project will also support the implementation of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).

6. The project will be implemented by a consortium of local and international organizations with expertise in climate change adaptation, water resources management, and community-based development. The consortium will work closely with local communities to ensure that the project is culturally sensitive and responsive to their needs.

7. The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial phase of one year for planning and preparation. The project will be funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) through the Adaptation Fund (AF).

8. The project will be implemented in close collaboration with the Government of Kenya, which is responsible for the overall management of the project. The project will also work closely with other stakeholders, including civil society organizations, the private sector, and local communities.

# B.1 Climate context (see: 1000 worlds, approximately 2 pages)

## Kenya suffers severely climatic stress

### Introduction

Kenya is one of the countries most affected by climate change. The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) has indicated that by 2030, Kenya will be severely impacted by the negative effects of climate change in the region. Much of the country's agricultural output depends on rainfall and temperature. Kenya was reported to have lost $1 billion due to drought between 2008 and 2017. The normal level of the sea season, Kenya's most important source of capital, has been affected by climate change. This has also affected the well-being of the country as a whole.

### Impact on Agriculture

Maize is one of Africa's major crops. In 2019, over 50% of maize was lost due to drought and pests. The impact of climate change on agriculture in Kenya is significant. The country relies heavily on rain-fed agriculture, making it vulnerable to the effects of climate change. According to the World Bank, Kenya has experienced a decrease in agricultural productivity by about 14% since 2000. This is due to changes in temperature and precipitation patterns.

### Temperature Trends

![Temperature Trend](image_url)

Figure 1: Time series chart for observed temperature

The average annual temperature in Kenya has increased by 1.3°C over the past century, with a significant increase of 0.5°C since 1960. This warming trend is expected to continue, reaching an additional 2-3°C by 2050 if current emission trends persist.

### Conclusion

Climate change poses a significant threat to Kenya's agricultural sector and overall economy. The country must adapt to these changes through improved farming practices, early warning systems for droughts and floods, and investment in climate-resilient infrastructure.

# ABDI River Catchment Area Project Location Baseline Assessment

11. **ABDI River Catchment Area (ABDIA)** located in South West Kenya, is one of the major water resources catchments in the country. It covers an area of approximately 2,608 km² and has been identified as a key source of water for the city of Nairobi. Even though the catchment is relatively small compared to other major river basins in Kenya, it is the main source of water for Nairobi and will be the main basin for Phase 1. Assuming the current modeling approach was accurate, the baseline assessment indicates that ABDIA has an estimated annual average flow of approximately 270 million cubic meters (MCM) per year based on the 2019 water resources of 1,983 MCM/year.

![Figure 1: Water basins and water balance in Kenya](image_url)

**Figure 1:** Water basins and water balance in Kenya

12. ABDIA is classified as a semi-arid catchment area with the upper basin area being classified as a forest reserve. The lower part of the catchment is mainly agricultural land used for farming. The main river flowing through ABDIA is the Athi River which flows into the Tana River at the Athi-Tana Delta. The catchment has an estimated population of 1,058,362 people and a total area of 2,608 km².

13. Metals and other pollutants in Nairobi's Upper ABDA and Coastal ABDA sub-basins, such as lead, cadmium, mercury, and arsenic, have been identified through various studies. These metals are associated with industrial activities, mining, and agricultural practices. The Upper ABDA catchment is part of the catchments of Nairobi River and its sub-basins, which include the Ngong River, Athi River, and other smaller tributaries. The Coastal ABDA catchment includes the Athi River and its sub-basins.

14. The ratio of 76% of water demand to water resources, which is called a water stress index, shows a very high level of water stress in ABDIA. This indicates that the water demand exceeds the available supply by a significant margin. It is projected that the water demand will increase to about 291% against the baseline assessment.

The most important week of the year is the dry season. This season is usually experienced by the African countries especially those that are located near the equator. The dry season is the period when there is no rainfall and the weather is hot and dry. This season is usually experienced in the months of November to April every year. # The problem in an All River Catchment is subordinate to climate risks due to the high dependence on natural resources for food, fuel and shelter according to USD (2013), which are and exacerbated by the impacts of climate change. This has led to increased vulnerability to droughts, economic disruption and social instability. This is often in the proximity of salt water intrusion. Such are some of the problems that have been identified as a result of climate change. The impact of climate change on the Upper Nile Basin has a major resource implication. Although the region has experienced severe droughts over the last decade, the impacts of these droughts have not been well documented. The impacts of climate change on the Upper Nile Basin are likely to be exacerbated by the increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events such as floods, storms and heat waves. These impacts will affect the livelihoods of people in the region, particularly those who depend on agriculture for their livelihoods.

The country's inland areas are largely and with two-thirds of the country receiving less than 500 mm of rainfall per year. The impact of climate change is likely to exacerbate the existing water scarcity problem. The impacts of climate change will be felt in the form of reduced rainfall, increased temperatures and changes in precipitation patterns. This will lead to increased evaporation and transpiration, which will reduce the amount of water available for agriculture and other uses. The impacts of climate change will also affect the availability of water for domestic use.

# Forest Management Practices

## Historical Background

### Early Twentieth Century

- Forests have quickly equated to sustainable management in recent years, including low-impact logging and forestry practices.
- Properly managed forests can provide long-term benefits for both humans and wildlife.

### Modern Era

- The use of modern technology has allowed for the development of new methods for managing forests.
- These methods are generally associated with higher temperatures on private lands.
- Forests have been increasingly recognized as important carbon sinks, helping to mitigate climate change.
- The management of these forests is often done in conjunction with other land uses.

## Recent Developments

### Global Conservation Models

- Recent advances in global conservation models have allowed for the development of new methods for managing forests.
- These models can be used to predict future changes in forest cover and biodiversity.
- The use of these models has been shown to be effective in predicting future changes in forest cover.

### Advanced Technologies

- Advances in remote sensing technology have allowed for the development of new methods for monitoring forest health.
- These technologies can be used to detect changes in forest cover and biodiversity.
- The use of these technologies has been shown to be effective in detecting changes in forest cover.

## Figures

![Figure 1](image1.png) ![Figure 2](image2.png)

### Figure 1: Historical Forest Data (circa 1900 - 2000)

- Shows the estimated net loss of forest cover over time.
- Highlights the impact of human activities on forest cover.

![Figure 3](image3.png) ![Figure 4](image4.png)

### Figure 3: Modern Forest Data (circa 2015 - 2040)

- Shows the estimated net gain in forest cover over time.
- Highlights the impact of modern conservation efforts on forest cover.

## Conclusion

- Forest management practices have evolved significantly over time.
- The use of modern technology has allowed for the development of new methods for managing forests.
- These methods can be used to predict future changes in forest cover and biodiversity.
- The use of these technologies has been shown to be effective in detecting changes in forest cover.

## The trend analysis of rainfall in Machakos Meteorological station indicates a decrease of annual and seasonal rainfall. This is attributed to the effects of climate change which have been observed over the last decade. The rainfall decreased by 7.5 mm annually, the same trend is observed in all the other seasons as careful weather monitoring has shown. The highest rainfall was recorded in October and November with an average of 100mm per month. The lowest rainfall was recorded in December and January with an average of 20mm per month. This trend is the same as recorded in the last decade. The October to February period is the wettest while March to September is the driest. This trend has been observed over the past few years and used as a representation of the ARCA, then it can be concluded that the rainfall with the Cashman decreases.

26. Karumanchi et al. highlight another way a changing climate could impact health. With increased air pollution, there are likely to be more respiratory diseases. The World Health Organization (WHO) estimated that 4.9 million deaths worldwide were attributable to outdoor air pollution in 2017. In the United States, the American Lung Association reported that 4 out of 10 people live in counties with unhealthy levels of ozone or particulate matter. The New South Wales Government (NSW), Australia, has implemented a range of measures to reduce air pollution and improve air quality. These include changes in fuel standards and vehicle emissions, the greening of urban areas, and the number of trees planted. In addition, NSW has introduced a range of measures to reduce water use, including water restrictions and incentives for water conservation. The state government has also implemented a range of measures to reduce energy consumption, such as the installation of solar panels on public buildings.

Along with the impact of precipitation and evaporation patterns in the study area, and its effects on soil moisture and runoff, one of the most important impacts of climate change on water systems is the influence on river flows, or the amount of water available to support agriculture, industry, and communities. Changes in rainfall and snowmelt can affect the timing and volume of streamflow, which can have significant implications for water availability and water quality. In addition, changes in precipitation patterns can also affect the frequency and intensity of floods and droughts, which can have significant impacts on water resources.

Climate change is likely to exacerbate water quality issues in the study areas. Extreme weather events such as floods and droughts can affect water quality through soil erosion, which further affects the water quality and quantity. These issues have reported the impact of increased sedimentation and suspended material from erosion. During the drought period, lower river flow can make the pollution can spread more easily, resulting in higher concentrations of pollutants. In addition, increased temperature can lead to increased nutrient loading, which may result in eutrophication. The design of water treatment plants should consider these potential impacts on water quality.

# C

Climate change can increase the spread of tropical fish into non-tropical seas, weaken and other marine life, and alter marine food chains. Coral reefs are particularly vulnerable to ocean warming because they depend on symbiotic algae for nutrition. The loss of these algae can lead to coral bleaching and death. Rising sea levels also threaten coastal communities by increasing the risk of flooding and storm surges.

Another example is the increase in the frequency and intensity of hurricanes, which can cause significant damage to coastal communities. In 2005, Hurricane Katrina caused catastrophic damage along the U.S. Gulf Coast, resulting in billions of dollars in property damage and hundreds of deaths. The increased frequency and intensity of such storms are expected to continue as a result of climate change.

From the above discussion, it has been established that temperature is increasing. The frequency of cold days has decreased while the frequency of hot days has increased. This trend is expected to continue in the future. As a result, the number of days suitable for outdoor activities will increase, and the number of days suitable for indoor activities will decrease.

Another challenge relates to the adaptive capacity of the impacts of climate change on the state of the ocean. As water temperatures rise, so does the frequency of harmful algal blooms (HABs). HABs are a natural phenomenon that can occur when certain types of algae grow out of control and produce toxins that can be harmful to marine life and humans.

Other challenges include the potential for increased ocean acidification, which can harm marine organisms such as corals and shellfish. Ocean acidification is caused by the absorption of carbon dioxide from the atmosphere into the ocean, leading to a decrease in the pH of seawater. This can make it difficult for some marine organisms to build and maintain their shells and skeletons.

# Climate Change Impacts & Challenges

## Key climate change impacts identified for the region

| Impact | Potential Consequences |
|--------|-----------------------|
| **Increased Temperature and Precipitation** | Increased frequency of extreme weather events (e.g., heatwaves, droughts) affecting agriculture, water resources, and human health. |
| **Sea Level Rise** | Increased coastal flooding, saltwater intrusion into freshwater aquifers, and impacts on coastal ecosystems. |
| **Changes in Water Availability** | Decreased water supply due to reduced snowpack and earlier snowmelt, impacting agriculture, hydropower, and municipal water supplies. |

## Sector-specific Impacts & Challenges

### Water Resources

#### Flooding

- Increased frequency of extreme weather events (e.g., hurricanes) leading to more intense flooding.
- Infrastructure damage from increased floodwaters.
- Saltwater intrusion into freshwater aquifers due to sea level rise.

#### Droughts

- Decreased water supply due to reduced snowpack and earlier snowmelt.
- Impacts on agriculture, hydropower, and municipal water supplies.
- Increased competition for limited water resources among different sectors.

### Ecosystems & Biodiversity

- Changes in species distribution and habitat suitability due to temperature changes.
- Increased frequency of extreme weather events (e.g., heatwaves) affecting plant and animal species.
- Saltwater intrusion into freshwater ecosystems, altering habitats and species composition.

### Human Health

- Increased incidence of heat-related illnesses due to higher temperatures.
- Changes in the timing and severity of allergy and asthma seasons.
- Impacts on mental health from extreme weather events (e.g., post-traumatic stress disorder).

### Infrastructure & Economy

- Damage to infrastructure from extreme weather events (e.g., flooding, wind damage).
- Increased maintenance costs for infrastructure due to more frequent and intense weather events.
- Economic impacts from disruptions in transportation, agriculture, and tourism.

## Project Support & Implementation

The project will support the implementation of the Arizona Nature Association Plan 2015-2020, which aims to enhance climate change adaptation efforts. The project will also contribute to the development of a regional climate change adaptation plan for the state. The project will be implemented in collaboration with local and regional stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and community groups.

# C

## 3.2. Theory of change (max. 1000 words, approximately 2 pages plus diagram)

The project seeks to enhance community resilience and water security in the Upper Aru River Catchment in Papua New Guinea through a multi-faceted approach. The theory of change is based on the premise that climate-induced droughts, floods, water scarcity, and other natural hazards will increasingly impact communities in the area. The project aims to build capacity among local government structures to address these impacts.

### Enhancing capacity

Local government and community-based organizations (CBOs) will be trained to manage flood and drought within the ARCA framework. This training will include:

- **Flood management:** Training on early warning systems, emergency response, and disaster risk reduction.
- **Drought management:** Training on water resource management, including rainwater harvesting and groundwater recharge.

### Strengthening local governance

Local governments will be supported to enhance their capacity to cope with climate change in water sector. This includes:

- **Capacity building:** Training on climate change adaptation planning, monitoring, and evaluation.
- **Data collection:** Support for collecting and analyzing data on climate change impacts and vulnerabilities.
- **Community engagement:** Facilitating community participation in decision-making processes.

### Improving water security

The project will work with communities to improve their access to safe drinking water. This includes:

- **Water supply systems:** Repairing and maintaining existing water supply systems, including wells and boreholes.
- **Rainwater harvesting:** Promoting rainwater harvesting as a means of increasing water availability during dry periods.

### Building resilience

The project will support communities to build their resilience to climate change by:

- **Disaster risk reduction:** Training on disaster risk reduction and emergency response.
- **Climate-smart agriculture:** Promoting climate-smart agricultural practices to enhance food security.
- **Early warning systems:** Establishing early warning systems for floods, droughts, and other natural hazards.

### Theory of change improved

The theory of change has been revised to include a clear chain of inputs, activities, outputs, and outcomes. The main outcomes are:

- **Enhanced capacity:** Local governments and CBOs have enhanced capacity to manage climate change impacts.
- **Improved water security:** Communities have improved access to safe drinking water.
- **Increased resilience:** Communities are more resilient to climate change.

# Objectives

- Strengthen water and ecosystem planning, management and regulatory framework to respond to changing climate conditions.

## Objective 1: Fund level inputs to the project are an increased reliance on I-1 infrastructure and the bulk enhancement to existing systems to address climate change impacts.
- The objective is to provide funding for the development of a climate change adaptation plan that will identify risks, vulnerabilities and needs in the water sector. This will include the identification of priority actions to reduce these risks.

## Objective 2: The first (pathway) to change (outcome 1) is designed to strengthen networks and facilitate the exchange of knowledge and information between different sectors.
- This objective aims to enhance collaboration among various stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and community groups. It will also promote the development of a shared vision for climate change adaptation in the water sector.

## Objective 3: The second (pathway) to change (outcome 2) identifies that time variability of water is impacted by climatic patterns of which human activity (including deforestation) has an impact.
- This objective focuses on understanding and addressing the impacts of climate change on water availability, including changes in precipitation patterns and increased frequency of extreme weather events. It aims to develop strategies for managing these risks.

## Objective 4: The third (pathway) to change (outcome 3) is strengthening capacity governance on water because the government has a responsibility to ensure that water is managed effectively.
- This objective emphasizes the importance of effective governance in water management, including the development of policies and regulations that support sustainable water use. It also aims to enhance the capacity of governments to manage water resources effectively.

## Implementation:
- The outcomes generated and the total investment of support for the project in order to address reliable climate change adaptation.
- State and provincial NEMA, WMA, FME, City government and community groups will be involved in the implementation of the planning documents. CEAFs, ICMPs and CMPs will be developed to ensure that the objectives are met.
- The regional governments will be responsible for the management and have enhanced compliance to the objectives.

## Objective 5: There is a theory of change for the project.

## B.3. Project/programme description (max. 2000 words, approximately 4 pages)

### Project objectives, outcomes and outputs

- **Objective 1:** To improve the quality of education in Mathematics, Science, Natural and Environmental Studies in the Upper Nile region.
    - **Outcome 1.1:** Improved teaching methods and materials.
        - **Output 1.1.1:** Development of teaching manuals.
        - **Output 1.1.2:** Training of teachers.
    - **Outcome 1.2:** Enhanced learning environment.
        - **Output 1.2.1:** Construction of classrooms.
        - **Output 1.2.2:** Provision of educational materials.

- **Objective 2:** To increase access to quality healthcare services in the region.
    - **Outcome 2.1:** Improved health infrastructure.
        - **Output 2.1.1:** Construction of health centers.
        - **Output 2.1.2:** Procurement of medical equipment.
    - **Outcome 2.2:** Enhanced healthcare services.
        - **Output 2.2.1:** Training of healthcare workers.
        - **Output 2.2.2:** Implementation of health programs.

### Project implementation strategy

- **Phase 1: Planning and Preparation**
    - Conduct a needs assessment.
    - Develop project plans and budgets.
    - Establish partnerships with local stakeholders.

- **Phase 2: Implementation**
    - Develop teaching manuals and materials.
    - Construct classrooms and health centers.
    - Procure medical equipment and educational materials.
    - Train teachers and healthcare workers.

- **Phase 3: Monitoring and Evaluation**
    - Monitor project progress.
    - Evaluate outcomes and outputs.
    - Adjust implementation strategies as needed.

### Project Management

- **Project Team:**
    - Project Manager
    - Education Specialist
    - Health Specialist
    - Finance Officer
    - Monitoring and Evaluation Officer

- **Partnerships:**
    - Local government agencies
    - Non-governmental organizations (NGOs)
    - Community-based organizations (CBOs)

### Project Timeline

- **Start Date:** January 20XX
- **End Date:** December 20XX

# C

The current approach to climate change mitigation is a regulatory-driven framework for sustainable production of food, fuel and goods that minimizes greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions and promotes adaptation to climate change. This approach is based on the assumption that the benefits of climate change mitigation will be distributed equitably among countries and communities.

## P.1. The benefits of climate change mitigation

The benefits of climate change mitigation are numerous, including:

- Reduced GHG emissions
- Improved air quality
- Increased energy efficiency
- Enhanced water management
- Improved public health
- Economic growth

These benefits can be realized through a variety of measures, such as:

- Energy efficiency improvements in buildings and industry
- Renewable energy adoption
- Carbon capture and storage (CCS)
- Improved land use practices
- Sustainable transportation

## P.2. The project of modern agriculture and its provision to the food security drawing water resources for the future

The project of modern agriculture is aimed at providing food security in the future. This project involves the development of new technologies and practices that can help to improve the efficiency of agricultural production. These technologies include:

- Precision agriculture
- Sustainable irrigation systems
- Improved crop varieties
- Integrated pest management
- Soil conservation techniques

These technologies can help to increase the productivity of agricultural systems, while also reducing the environmental impact of agriculture.

## P.3. The project of modern agriculture and its provision to the food security drawing water resources for the future

The project of modern agriculture is aimed at providing food security in the future. This project involves the development of new technologies and practices that can help to improve the efficiency of agricultural production. These technologies include:

- Precision agriculture
- Sustainable irrigation systems
- Improved crop varieties
- Integrated pest management
- Soil conservation techniques

These technologies can help to increase the productivity of agricultural systems, while also reducing the environmental impact of agriculture.

## Table 4: Examples of climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies for crops 3

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| Early warning systems | Systems that provide timely and accurate information on weather conditions. |
| Crop diversification | Growing a variety of crops to reduce the risk of crop failure due to adverse weather conditions. |
| Improved irrigation systems | Efficient use of water for irrigation, including drip irrigation and sprinkler systems. |
| Climate-smart agriculture | Practices that increase the resilience of agricultural systems to climate change. |

# WRAA Budget for FY2018

WRAA has capacity of over 700 staff. The Authority is governed by a board of Directors consisting of a Chairman and a semi-autonomous staff headed by a Chief Executive Officer. The Technical Coordination Unit (TCU) is the technical arm of WRAA, which is responsible for the overall management of the Authority’s projects.

WRAA has been both-based, operating in accordance with the National Water Resources Strategy in Management of Water Resources in Kenya. The TCU is responsible for the planning and implementation of water resources development projects. The TCU is also responsible for the coordination of all water resources activities within the country, including the monitoring and evaluation of water resources projects.

### Section 6.1: WRAA Budget for FY2018

Section 6.1 of the Water Act, 2016 provides for the budget of completed waterworks to the county government jointly with the national government. The Authority will be responsible for the management and maintenance of water resources within the country. The Authority will also be responsible for the development and implementation of water resources projects in the country.

The Authority will be responsible for the management of the water resources in the country, including the development and implementation of water resources projects in the country. The Authority will also be responsible for the management of the water resources in the country, including the development and implementation of water resources projects in the country.

### Section 6.2: Kenya Meteorological Department (KMD)

The Kenya Meteorological Department (KMD), a department under the Ministry of Environment and Forestry with the overall function of provision of meteorological and climatological services to various government sectors for better planning, management, and decision-making processes. KMD is responsible for the collection, processing, and dissemination of weather and climate information.

KMD is a department within the Ministry of Environment and Forestry and therefore it doesn’t have a legal entity as an independent department. The Department is headed by the Director of Meteorological Services supported by 5 Deputy Directors who head various sections in the department. KMD has offices in Nairobi, Kisumu, Mombasa, Eldoret, and Kitale.

### Justification for GCF funding request (max. 1000 words, approximately 2 pages)

**8.5. Justification**

Green finance is required from the GCF to enhance adaptive capacity of the communities in the upper Antiguan watershed and to reduce the risk of flooding. The Government of Antigua and Barbuda has committed to achieving the goals of the Paris Agreement. Main Plan 6 is not sufficient to achieve these goals on its own, hence additional funding is key to accelerate progress.

The proposed project purposes to create a clear branch of disaster charge management practices. This will be accomplished through the following activities:

- Conduct regular water quality monitoring.
- Implement water safety plans.
- Develop and implement a national knowledge management strategy.
- Conduct research on social cohesion.
- Conduct an on-Enrolled Entity audit.
- Implement a risk management framework.

These activities will enhance the effectiveness of their programs, ensure safe drinking water for consumers, inform policy decisions, and reduce the risk of flooding. The County Environmental Action Plan (CEAP), National Adaptation Plan (NAP), and other relevant documents have been reviewed to ensure alignment with national priorities.

### Project ID: P087

# C

## 108

This project will be led and implemented by local authorities including the Water Resources Ministry and its KNWRA partners to ensure sustainability. It will also involve relevant line ministries and other stakeholders in the implementation of the project. The technical assistance provided by WMA will be based on demand-driven approaches. This will include capacity building, training, and the development of a project management framework for the project. The project will also benefit from the experience gained during the preparation of the application of the WRAU funds as part of the Operation and Maintenance (O&M) component.

## 109

KNWRA and WMA-led field visits explore opportunities to ensure sustainability beyond the project period. The KNWRA will continue to work with the Water Resources Ministry to ensure that the project is implemented in a manner that is sustainable and leads to the benefits of the project being realized at the local level.

## 110

Additionally, Water Resource Users Associations (WRUAs) established by WMA are a vehicle for sustainability. WRUAs will continue to be supported through training and capacity building initiatives. This will ensure that the project is sustainable beyond the project period. WRUAs will also continue to play an important role in the management of water resources.

## 111

Close engagement with local government structures at community level

## 112

Close engagement with local government structures at community level

### Strengthened institutional capacity and presence

The project will strengthen the capacity of WRUAs to ensure sustainability. This will include training, mentoring, and technical support for WRUAs. The project will also work with local governments to build their capacity to manage water resources. This will help to ensure that the project is sustainable beyond the project period.

### Established feedback mechanisms

The project will establish a feedback mechanism to enable the users concerned to report to their relevant authorities. This feedback mechanism will contribute to long-term sustainability.

## Operation and Maintenance

### 114

During the implementation and after completion of the project, operation and maintenance (O&M) activities will be carried out by the local communities with support from KNWRA. The O&M activities will be funded through a combination of community contributions and government funding. KNWRA will continue to provide technical assistance to ensure that the project is sustainable.

### 115

To this end, the project will work closely with the local communities to ensure that they are able to manage their water resources in a sustainable manner. ## D

This section refers to the performance of the project/programme against the intended outcome as set out in D.

### D.1 Impact potential index (500 words, approximately 1 page)

Expected increased reliance on healthy and wellbeing, and food and water security
- The project is expected to increase the resilience of communities to climate change through improved access to clean water, sanitation, and hygiene services. This will lead to an increase in agricultural productivity and income levels. The project aims to strengthen community-based adaptation initiatives that are resilient to climate change. The project also aims to enhance the capacity of local governments to implement climate change mitigation and adaptation measures.

Expected strengthening of institutional and regulatory systems for climate-resilient planning and development for A & B
- The project will support the development of national policies, regulations, and guidelines for climate resilience in agriculture, water resources management, and disaster risk reduction. This will include the establishment of a national climate change policy framework that is consistent with international best practices.

Expected increase in generation and use of climate information in decision-making PPMF A & B and climate indices
- The project aims to boost climate-resilient data collection in vulnerable countries and enhance long-term climate services. It will support the development of climate information systems, including early warning systems, that are tailored to the needs of local communities.

Expected strengthening of adaptive capacity and enhanced resilience to climate risks PPMF A & B and climate indices
- The use of climate information planning for water resources and other developed governance is expected to increase the resilience of vulnerable communities. This will include the development of climate-smart agriculture practices, improved water management, and early warning systems.

Expected strengthening of awareness of climate threats and risk reduction processes PPMF A & B and climate indices
- The project aims to build the capacity of local institutions including NEMA, WRA, KMD and County governments to enhance their ability to respond to climate change. This will include the development of a national climate change policy framework that is consistent with international best practices.

Death tolls from droughts are expected to be lower as water points will all develop participatory in the project and other communities.

### D.2 Partnerships and funding

Partnerships will be established with local, regional, and national governments to ensure the effective implementation of climate action and ecosystem restoration. A hybrid approach is required to ensure that the project is implemented effectively.

## D

Existing commitments including funding, expertise, creation, skills and knowledge to implement climate-smart water resources management and climate change adaptation measures.

### D.2 Contribution to Regulatory Framework and Policies

#### 120
The Proposed Project will contribute to the implementation of operationalization of several key national policies and strategies that are aligned with the National Climate Change Policy (NCCP), National Adaptation Plan (NAP), and the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) and the Paris Agreement. The project will also support the implementation of the water sector policy and strategic action plan for climate change adaptation.

#### 121
The project will contribute to the implementation of the water sector policy and strategic action plan for climate change adaptation, which is aligned with the NAP, NDCs, and the Paris Agreement. The project will also support the development of a comprehensive climate change adaptation strategy for the water sector that is consistent with the objectives of the NAP.

### D.3 Sustainable Development Potential

#### Whole benefits and profits

##### 124
The project will contribute to the achievement of several sustainable development goals, including:

- **Goal 6: Clean Water and Sanitation** - The project will improve access to clean water for domestic use and irrigation.
- **Goal 7: Affordable and Clean Energy** - The project will increase energy efficiency in water pumping systems.
- **Goal 13: Climate Action** - The project will contribute to climate change mitigation by reducing greenhouse gas emissions from water pumping systems.

#### 125
The project will also contribute to the achievement of several other sustainable development goals, including:

- **Goal 1: No Poverty** - The project will create jobs and improve livelihoods for local communities.
- **Goal 3: Good Health and Well-being** - The project will improve access to clean water for drinking and sanitation, reducing the risk of waterborne diseases.
- **Goal 5: Gender Equality** - The project will promote gender equality by involving women in the implementation and management of the project.

#### 126
The project will also contribute to the achievement of several other sustainable development goals, including:

- **Goal 9: Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure** - The project will improve water infrastructure and promote innovation in climate-smart water management.
- **Goal 11: Sustainable Cities and Communities** - The project will improve access to clean water for urban areas, promoting sustainable cities and communities.

#### 127
The project will also contribute to the achievement of several other sustainable development goals, including:

- **Goal 14: Life Below Water** - The project will promote sustainable use of water resources and protect aquatic ecosystems.
- **Goal 15: Life on Land** - The project will promote sustainable use of land and water resources.

#### 128
The project will also contribute to the achievement of several other sustainable development goals, including:

- **Goal 16: Peace, Justice, and Strong Institutions** - The project will promote good governance and strengthen institutions for climate change adaptation.
- **Goal 17: Partnerships for the Goals** - The project will promote international cooperation and partnerships for climate change adaptation.

Kenya has nearly been selected among countries to implement Human Rights Based Approach (HRBA) in their climate change projects. This project aims to mainstream HRBA into the country’s climate change policies and programs. The project will be implemented through a multi-stakeholder approach that includes government, civil society, private sector, and communities. The project will also support the development of a gender-responsive climate change policy and action plan.

### Mission: Deforestation activities in the study area MA has become a concern at local level through NWMILA, with steps to integrate these activities at country level. The Government will collaborate with other stakeholders, NGOs and INGOs to address this issue.

Kenya Forest Service (KFS) is the lead agency for forest conservation and management of forest resources in Kenya. KFS has been involved in the National Reducing Emissions from Deforestation and forest Degradation (REDD+) initiative. The KFS has also been involved in the development of the National Greenhouse Gas Inventory (GHGI) and the National Forest Reference Emission Levels (FREL).

### D.6 Application of best practices
The project will apply best practices in climate change adaptation and mitigation. These include:

- **Community Engagement**: The project will engage local communities through participatory planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.
- **Gender Equality**: The project will promote gender equality by ensuring equal participation of women and men in all activities.
- **Environmental Management**: The project will adopt best practices for environmental management to minimize negative impacts on the environment.

### 106
The project will also benefit from similar projects from the world including Bolivia, Brazil, and others. These projects have been successful in implementing climate change adaptation measures. The project team will learn from these experiences and adapt them to the local context.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 14 OF 73

## Table of Contents
- Context of the request
- Key options and methodologies
- Results, benefits, risks
- Indicators
- Implementation arrangements
- Monitoring framework and reporting framework
- Education

## Context of the request
- **Context:** The request is for funding to support climate change mitigation activities.
- **Objective:** To reduce greenhouse gas emissions through sustainable practices.

## Key options and methodologies
- **Option 1:** Implement sustainable agriculture practices.
- **Option 2:** Develop renewable energy projects.
- **Option 3:** Promote energy efficiency in industries.

## Results, benefits, risks
- **Benefits:**
  - Reduced greenhouse gas emissions.
  - Improved air quality.
  - Enhanced food security.
- **Risks:**
  - Financial constraints.
  - Technological challenges.
  - Political instability.

## Indicators
- **Indicator 1:** Number of sustainable agriculture practices adopted (SAPs).
- **Indicator 2:** Amount of renewable energy generated.
- **Indicator 3:** Energy efficiency improvements in industries.

## Implementation arrangements
- **Responsible entity:** Ministry of Environment and Climate Change.
- **Timeline:**
  - Phase 1: Planning and preparation (6 months).
  - Phase 2: Implementation (24 months).
  - Phase 3: Monitoring and evaluation (6 months).

## Monitoring framework and reporting framework
- **Frequency:** Quarterly reports.
- **Reporting format:** Digital and printed reports.

## Education
- **Objective:** To raise awareness about climate change and sustainable practices.
- **Activities:**
  - Workshops for farmers on sustainable agriculture.
  - Public awareness campaigns.
  - School programs on environmental conservation.

## E&A Activities

| Activity       | Description                                                                                       | Sub-activity   | Definitions                                                                 |
|---------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Workshop      | Conduct workshops with local communities to educate about climate change                         | Workshops      | Number of workshops conducted                             |
| SAPs          | Adopt sustainable agriculture practices (SAPs)                                                  | SAPs           | Number of SAPs implemented and fully operational              |
| Renewable     | Develop renewable energy projects                                                                   | Projects       | Number of renewable energy projects completed                 |
| Efficiency    | Promote energy efficiency in industries                                                           | Initiatives    | Number of initiatives implemented                            |

## Activity 1.1.1: Support for the development of a National Intended Contribution (NIC) and Low Emission Development Strategy (LEDS)

- A National Intended Contribution Committee will be established to guide the process.
- The NIC will be developed in consultation with relevant stakeholders, including government ministries, civil society organizations, and private sector entities.

## Activity 1.2: Support the development of a National Adaptation Plan (NAP) process

- The NAP process will be guided by the principles of gender equality, social inclusion, and climate justice.
- It will include the development of a roadmap for implementing adaptation measures.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 18 OF 73

## Activity 1.3.1: Enhance awareness and capacity building for climate change adaptation and mitigation

- **Objective:** To enhance awareness and capacity building for climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- **Description:** This activity aims to increase knowledge and understanding of climate change impacts, risks, vulnerability, and adaptation measures among key stakeholders.

## Activity 2.1: Support development of national communications and biennial update reports (BURs)

- **Objective:** To support the development of national communications and biennial update reports.
- **Description:** This activity will provide technical assistance to developing countries in preparing their national communications and BURs, ensuring compliance with the requirements of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

## Activity 3.1: Conduct workshops and capacity building initiatives

- **Objective:** To conduct workshops and capacity building initiatives.
- **Description:** This activity will organize workshops and training sessions to enhance the skills and knowledge of stakeholders in climate change adaptation and mitigation.

## Activity 4.2.1: Reinforce national institutions' capacities for climate change adaptation and mitigation

- **Objective:** To reinforce national institutions' capacities for climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- **Description:** This activity aims to strengthen the capacity of national institutions to effectively implement climate change adaptation and mitigation measures, including policy development and implementation.

## Activity 5.1: Enhance understanding of climate change impacts on ecosystems and biodiversity

- **Objective:** To enhance understanding of climate change impacts on ecosystems and biodiversity.
- **Description:** This activity will support research and data collection efforts to better understand the impacts of climate change on ecosystems and biodiversity, including the identification of vulnerable species and habitats.

## Activity 6.1: Support development of national adaptation plans (NAPs)

- **Objective:** To support the development of national adaptation plans.
- **Description:** This activity will provide technical assistance to developing countries in the preparation and implementation of their NAPs, ensuring alignment with national development priorities and international commitments.

## Activity 7.2.1: Promote climate-smart agriculture practices

- **Objective:** To promote climate-smart agriculture practices.
- **Description:** This activity aims to enhance the resilience of agricultural systems to climate change by promoting climate-smart agriculture practices, including improved crop varieties, sustainable water management, and integrated pest management.

## Activity 8.1: Support development of renewable energy projects

- **Objective:** To support the development of renewable energy projects.
- **Description:** This activity will provide technical assistance and financing for the development of renewable energy projects, including solar, wind, hydro, and geothermal power.

## Activity 9.0.E: Conduct workshops to develop the NDCs

- **Objective:** To conduct workshops to develop the NDCs.
- **Description:** This activity aims to support developing countries in the preparation and submission of their Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) through a series of capacity-building workshops.

## Activity 10.1: Enhance understanding of climate change impacts on water resources

- **Objective:** To enhance understanding of climate change impacts on water resources.
- **Description:** This activity will support research and data collection efforts to better understand the impacts of climate change on water resources, including changes in precipitation patterns, groundwater levels, and river flows.

## Activity 11.1: Support development of climate-resilient infrastructure

- **Objective:** To support the development of climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **Description:** This activity aims to enhance the resilience of infrastructure systems to climate change by supporting the integration of climate change considerations into infrastructure planning, design, and construction.

## Activity 12.1: Enhance understanding of climate change impacts on ecosystems

- **Objective:** To enhance understanding of climate change impacts on ecosystems.
- **Description:** This activity will support research and data collection efforts to better understand the impacts of climate change on ecosystems, including changes in species distribution, habitat degradation, and ecosystem services.

## Activity 13.0.E: Support development of climate-resilient infrastructure

- **Objective:** To support the development of climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **Description:** This activity aims to enhance the resilience of infrastructure systems to climate change by supporting the integration of climate change considerations into infrastructure planning, design, and construction.

## Activity 14.0.E: Support development of climate-resilient infrastructure

- **Objective:** To support the development of climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **Description:** This activity aims to enhance the resilience of infrastructure systems to climate change by supporting the integration of climate change considerations into infrastructure planning, design, and construction.

- **Description:** This activity aims to enhance the resilience of infrastructure systems to climate change by supporting the integration of climate change considerations into infrastructure planning, design, and construction.

## F. Environmental and Social Management System

The environmental and social management system will guarantee the continuing sound, safe, and reliable use of the established assets during their useful life and ensure proper operation and maintenance to promote the benefits for the people working throughout the project lifespan.

### MAE Project

The MAE project will be undertaken using participatory methodologies, especially by the EES, enhance continuous learning and promoting a sustainable project. The MAE will be implemented through the following activities:

- **Environmental Management Plan (EMP)**: This plan will be developed to ensure that the environmental impacts of the project are properly managed.
- **Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS)**: This system will be established to ensure that the project is conducted in accordance with the EES. The ESMS will include a monitoring system for the implementation of the EMP, ensuring compliance with the environmental requirements set by the MAE plan.

### Activity: Reviewing/Processing Documentation

This activity includes reviewing and updating all possible documents on an accomplishment of the general activities under each Output and the specific activities under each Component. This is to ensure that all the required documentation is up-to-date and properly maintained for future reference.

- **Output 1**: Processing Environmental Performance Assessment
  - The primary project environmental performance assessment will be conducted by the team to ensure proper implementation.
  - **Foresight**: This activity will be carried out in accordance with the project document, ensuring that all environmental requirements are met.
  - **Description**: This activity will include a review of the environmental impact assessment (EIA) and other relevant documents. The team will also conduct an analysis to ensure compliance with the environmental regulations.

### Risk Management

The risk management system will be implemented to identify potential risks and develop strategies to mitigate them. This includes:

- **Risk Identification**: Identifying potential risks that may affect the project.
- **Risk Assessment**: Assessing the likelihood and impact of identified risks.
- **Risk Mitigation**: Developing strategies to minimize the impact of identified risks.

### Output 2: Risk Factors and Mitigations Measures

| Risk Factor | Probability | Impact |
|-------------|--------------|--------|
| Foresight   | High         | Medium |

#### Description
This activity will include a review of all risk factors and their corresponding mitigation measures. The team will conduct an analysis to identify potential risks and develop strategies to mitigate them.

## Table 8: Gender Assessment and Social Management plan

### G.2 Gender assessment and action plan (max 500 words, approximately 1 page)

**169.** The gender assessment focuses on processes and procedures that ensure the gender balance in the project implementation team with the commitment areas. A thorough gender analysis using qualitative methods was employed to identify the gender-related issues and challenges faced by women and men in the project area. The assessment also considered the roles of women and men in decision-making, land ownership, and access to resources.

**170.** The strategic environmental management plan includes gender-specific activities that address the identified gender-related issues and challenges. These include capacity-building efforts and awareness-raising campaigns on gender equality and climate change adaptation. Low-emission development strategies are also promoted. Particular attention is given to the needs of vulnerable groups, such as women, children, and persons with disabilities.

**171.** The gender action plan aims to ensure that the project benefits both men and women equally. It includes specific actions to address the identified gender-related issues and challenges. These actions are designed to promote gender equality and empower women and girls. The plan also includes monitoring and evaluation mechanisms to track progress towards achieving gender equality.

**172.** The gender action plan is developed in consultation with local communities, including women's groups and other stakeholders. It is based on the findings of the gender assessment and incorporates the recommendations made by the community. The plan is reviewed regularly to ensure that it remains relevant and effective.

**173.** The gender action plan is integrated into the overall project implementation plan. This ensures that gender-related issues are addressed throughout the project cycle, from planning to monitoring and evaluation.

### G.3 Financial management and procurement (max 600 words, approximately 1 page)

**174.** NEMA is the executing entity for the implementation of financial management procedures in line with NEMA's internal policies and guidelines. The financial management plan includes budgeting, accounting, and reporting procedures that ensure transparency and accountability.

**175.** The procurement process follows NEMA's procurement policy and guidelines. It ensures that all procurements are conducted in a fair, transparent, and competitive manner. All procurement activities are documented and reported to NEMA's internal audit team.

**176.** The financial management plan includes provisions for the proper use of funds and the prevention of fraud and corruption. It also ensures compliance with relevant laws and regulations.

## TSA

NEMA shall have scope with the national executing framework to ensure the Programme is aligned with the National Climate Change Response Strategy and the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) as well as the Paris Agreement.

Additionally, NEMA shall be a technical committee entity under the Adaptation Fund Board. The board has the main mandate standards concerning transparency and accountability. The operation of the board will also play a key role in ensuring that the funds are used effectively and efficiently.

NEMA has been a leading host for various climate change conferences and meetings. This includes:

- NEMA hosts a Consultant and Offers
  - NEMA has hosted several consultancy firms to provide technical support and the programme requires that NEMA continues to engage with these consultants.
  - NEMA also provides consultancy services and the programme requires that NEMA continues to provide these services.

- NEMA has a Climate Risk and Mitigation Plan that is under implementation.
  - The plan was developed in collaboration with the Government of Canada (GoC) and the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).
  - The plan was developed through a consultative process involving various stakeholders including the GoC, UNDP, NEMA and other relevant entities.

- NEMA also has a climate unit seconded from the National Police Service, who largely monitor environmental compliance.

# Secretariat’s assessment of FP175

## Proposal name:
Enhancing community resilience and water security in the Upper Tana River Basin through integrated water resource management

## Accepted entity:
National Environment Management Authority (NEMA)

## Country(ies):
Kenya

## Project/programme title:
Miro

## I. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### 1. Strengths and weaknesses

| Strengths | Points of concern |
| --- | --- |
| Linking biodiversity, ecosystem services and water resource management with community needs and aspirations (NEAP) and Kenya’s Vision 2030 and Big Four Agenda. | Operations and implementation (M&E of capacity building): The project has not yet been implemented on the ground but is expected to be implemented in the coming months. |
| Strong stakeholder engagement and support from local communities, national government and other stakeholders. | Monitoring and evaluation: The project has not yet been implemented on the ground but is expected to be implemented in the coming months. |
| Community participation in DSM of water resources and integrated water resource management (IWRM) through a joint cost of conservation. | Operations and implementation (M&E of capacity building): The project has not yet been implemented on the ground but is expected to be implemented in the coming months. |

### 2. Comments

The Board may wish to consider approaching this funding proposal with the view and conditions that it is subject to the following:

- The project is implemented under the right (green) and conditions and recommendations.

## II. Summary of the Secretariat’s assessment

### 2.1 Project background

Kenya’s youth and women are highly vulnerable to water availabilities, thus making water security a key issue for the country. Water security concerns in Kenya are compounded by climate change impacts.


---
**Page 82**
---

# Green Climate Fund

## Project Title: Kenya Upper Athi River Catchment Area

### Objective
To climate-proof lands which directly impact on the quantity and quality of the catchment water resources. Kenya has identified biodiversity and water scarcity along with additional threats such as deforestation, pollution, and overgrazing.

### Project Description
Among Kenya's upper catchment areas, the Athi River Catchment is a critical source of water for Nairobi City and its environs. The project aims to address climate change impacts on the 12 counties that depend on this catchment area. These include Kajiado, Machakos, Makueni, Kitui, Embu, Kirinyaga, Murang'a, Nyeri, Nyandarua, Nakuru, Laikipia, and Meru.

In the target project areas, most households have limited access to improved water sources with some relying on unprotected springs. In addition, climate change has impacted groundwater resources, leading to increased water scarcity and reduced water quality. The project will support the development of climate-resilient water management systems in the Athi River Catchment area.

### Project Components
1. **Component 1: Water Resource Management**
   - Objective: To improve water resource management.
   - Activities: This includes improving water storage, distribution, and use efficiency; promoting integrated water resources management (IWRM); and enhancing community participation in water resource management.

2. **Component 2: Climate-Resilient Agriculture**
   - Objective: To enhance climate resilience of agriculture.
   - Activities: This involves promoting climate-smart agricultural practices; improving access to weather information and early warning systems; and supporting the development of climate-resilient value chains.

3. **Component 3: Capacity Building and Knowledge Management**
   - Objective: To build capacity for climate change adaptation and mitigation.
   - Activities: This includes training and capacity building for communities, local governments, and other stakeholders; promoting knowledge sharing and learning; and enhancing the use of climate information in decision-making.

### Implementation
The project is categorized as a B level project under GCF's classification system. It will be implemented by the Government of Kenya through the Ministry of Environment and Forestry (MoEF) and the National Treasury.

### Funding
The project is funded with USD 32 million from the Green Climate Fund, with co-financing from the Government of Kenya.

### Component-by-Component Analysis

#### Component 1: Water Resource Management
- Objective: To improve water resource management.
- Activities:
  - Improving water storage and distribution infrastructure.
  - Promoting integrated water resources management (IWRM).
  - Enhancing community participation in water resource management.

#### Component 2: Climate-Resilient Agriculture
- Objective: To enhance climate resilience of agriculture.
- Activities:
  - Promoting climate-smart agricultural practices.
  - Improving access to weather information and early warning systems.
  - Supporting the development of climate-resilient value chains.

#### Component 3: Capacity Building and Knowledge Management
- Objective: To build capacity for climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- Activities:
  - Training and capacity building for communities, local governments, and other stakeholders.
  - Promoting knowledge sharing and learning.
  - Enhancing the use of climate information in decision-making.

### Conclusion
The project aims to facilitate climate-resilient development in water and agricultural lands across the Athi River Catchment area. It will be implemented by the Government of Kenya through the Ministry of Environment and Forestry, with funding from the Green Climate Fund.

## 3.1 Impact Potential

The estimated direct and indirect benefits of the community within the Kaka River Catchment are in relation to climate variability and change. The benefits for water for irrigation and livestock will be enhanced by the improved water resource management in the catchment. Five out of 12 communities (Kokota, Machakos, Masinga, Mavoko, and Athi River) have been identified as the most vulnerable to climate change impacts. The project's feasibility study on the analysis of the climatic and hydrological cycles at the Athi River Basin has shown that the projected increasing temperature and rainfall variability will affect the water balance and water availability in the region. The proposed interventions will improve the water balance situation of the Athi River Basin. The proposed interventions by the project will also enhance the livelihoods of the communities through improved water resource management with climate vulnerability and change.

## I. Assessment of the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

### 1.1 Impact potential

#### 1.1.1 Adapting impact

**Summary of reducing the impact** through the Upper Athi River Catchment Area (UARCA) project, which aims to improve water quality and quantity in the Athi River Basin. The project includes:

- **Technical features**: Installation of 25 automatic weather stations, 30 rain gauges, and 10 stream flow monitoring stations.
- **Water quality monitoring**: Regular monitoring of water quality parameters such as temperature, pH, conductivity, turbidity, and temperature.
- **Adaptive management**: Implementation of an adaptive management framework to guide the project’s implementation and ensure its effectiveness.

**Project components**:

- **Water harvesting systems**: Installation of a variety of water harvesting systems including sand dams, earth dams, and rainwater harvesting tanks.
- **Soil conservation**: Implementation of soil conservation measures such as terracing, contour farming, and planting of cover crops to prevent erosion.
- **Reforestation**: Planting of trees along riverbanks and in degraded areas to improve soil stability and water retention.

**Project objectives**:

- Improve water quality and quantity in the Athi River Basin.
- Enhance the livelihoods of local communities by providing access to clean water and improving agricultural productivity.
- Protect and restore the ecosystem services provided by the Upper Athi River Catchment Area.

**Project timeline**: The project is expected to be completed within a period of 5 years, starting from July 2021 and ending in June 2026. The project will be implemented in phases, with each phase lasting for approximately one year.

## AIMS AND OBJECTIVES

### 1. Activities

1. Although there are no specific activities aimed at modifying the regulatory framework, activity 3.1 would support strengthening the authority's ability to contract compliance with environmental standards.
2. Regarding projects, this project would support the development of County Environmental Impact Assessment and Environmental Management Plans.

## 1.1 Socio-economic benefits

### 1.1.1 Employment and capacity building

1. The project will create temporary employment for the youth residing in the catchment building that would be retained at the normal level for the NHIA, WMA and the Green Monitoring Project.
2. With respect to replanting, the project will replicate the experience of Kenya and Tanzania in creating permanent jobs through environmental conservation.

## 1.3 Sustainable development potential

### 1.3.1 Environmental co-benefits

1. The environmental co-benefit of the project would arise from activity 1.2, which would reduce greenhouse gas emissions by reducing deforestation and degradation. This would include:
   - Reducing carbon dioxide emissions by approximately 50 tons per year.
   - Reducing methane emissions by approximately 3 tons per year.
   - Reducing nitrous oxide emissions by approximately 1 ton per year.

2. Potential environmental co-benefits include improved biodiversity, reduced soil erosion and increased water retention. The project would also reduce the risk of wildfires, which can cause significant damage to forests and wildlife. This would also reduce the need for fire-fighting equipment and personnel, as well as the associated costs.
3. The project would also contribute to the achievement of the Aichi Biodiversity Targets, including Target 5 (Halting the loss of biodiversity) and Target 14 (Conserving marine and coastal biodiversity).

### 1.3.2 Social co-benefits

1. The social co-benefit of the project would arise from activity 1.2, which would reduce greenhouse gas emissions by reducing deforestation and degradation. This would include:
   - Reducing carbon dioxide emissions by approximately 50 tons per year.
   - Reducing methane emissions by approximately 3 tons per year.
   - Reducing nitrous oxide emissions by approximately 1 ton per year.

2. Potential social co-benefits include improved biodiversity, reduced soil erosion and increased water retention. The project would also reduce the risk of wildfires, which can cause significant damage to forests and wildlife. This would also reduce the need for fire-fighting equipment and personnel, as well as the associated costs.
3. The project would also contribute to the achievement of the Aichi Biodiversity Targets, including Target 5 (Halting the loss of biodiversity) and Target 14 (Conserving marine and coastal biodiversity).

The All-India average temperature is steadily increasing due to climate change. The average temperature in India has increased by 0.7°C over the past 100 years. The increase in temperature is more pronounced in the northern regions of the country. The average temperature in the northern region of the country has increased by 1.2°C over the past 100 years. The All-India average rainfall is steadily decreasing due to climate change. The average rainfall in India has decreased by 6% over the past 50 years. The decrease in rainfall is more pronounced in the northern regions of the country. The average rainfall in the northern region of the country has decreased by 12% over the past 50 years. The decrease in rainfall is more pronounced in the summer months.

# Greenpeace Report

## Study on Southern Right Whales (Eubalaena australis)

Recent studies highlight significant influences on the overall classification of the Eastern Subspecies in relation to their genetic and morphological characteristics. The study validates existing CHIRP (Cable-Sonar) data, revealing that Southern Right Whales are genetically distinct from other Eubalaena species. This distinction is supported by a comprehensive analysis of genetic markers and morphological traits.

The study was conducted using CHIRP (Cable-Sonar) data collected over the period 2011-2040, focusing on Eubalaena australis. The research indicates that Southern Right Whales are genetically distinct from other Eubalaena species due to their unique genetic markers and morphological traits.

The study also highlights the importance of understanding the genetic diversity within the species. This is crucial for conservation efforts as it helps in identifying potential threats and developing strategies to protect the species. The findings suggest that there may be a need to reclassify the species based on their genetic differences.

### Key Findings

- **Genetic Distinction**: Southern Right Whales are genetically distinct from other Eubalaena species.
- **Morphological Traits**: Unique morphological traits support the genetic distinction.
- **Conservation Implications**: Understanding genetic diversity is crucial for conservation efforts.

The study was published in the journal "Marine Mammal Science" and was led by researchers from the University of California, Santa Cruz. The findings have significant implications for the classification and conservation of Southern Right Whales.

### Conclusion

The study highlights the importance of genetic and morphological data in understanding the diversity within species. It also underscores the need for further research to better understand the genetic structure of Southern Right Whales and develop effective conservation strategies.

# Green Climate Fund

## Project Title

### Project Description

The project aims to address climate change mitigation and adaptation challenges by implementing various activities. Key components include:

- **Renewable Energy**: Promoting renewable energy sources such as solar and wind power.
- **Energy Efficiency**: Improving energy efficiency in buildings and industries.
- **Afforestation and Reforestation**: Planting trees to absorb carbon dioxide.

### Project Objectives

1. Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% through renewable energy projects.
2. Increase energy efficiency by 15% in residential and commercial buildings.
3. Plant one million trees annually to combat deforestation.

## Project Implementation

### Activities

1. **Renewable Energy Development**
   - Install solar panels on public buildings.
   - Set up wind turbines in suitable areas.
   - Provide subsidies for households to install renewable energy systems.

2. **Energy Efficiency Programs**
   - Conduct energy audits for businesses and homes.
   - Implement energy-efficient lighting and appliances.
   - Offer training programs for technicians to maintain and repair energy-efficient equipment.

3. **Afforestation and Reforestation**
   - Plant trees in deforested areas.
   - Establish community-based forest management programs.
   - Conduct regular monitoring of planted forests.

### Target Beneficiaries

- **Local Communities**: Directly benefit from renewable energy projects and reforestation efforts.
- **Youth and Women**: Given priority in employment and training opportunities.
- **Small and Medium Enterprises (SMEs)**: Supported with grants and technical assistance for energy efficiency improvements.

## Project Management

### Key Personnel

1. **Project Manager**
   - Responsible for overall project coordination and implementation.
2. **Technical Experts**
   - Specialists in renewable energy, energy efficiency, and forestry.
3. **Financial Advisors**
   - Manage budgeting and financial reporting.

### Implementation Timeline

- **Phase I (Year 1-3)**: Focus on planning, capacity building, and initial implementation of activities.
- **Phase II (Year 4-7)**: Scale up activities and ensure sustainability.
- **Phase III (Year 8-10)**: Monitor, evaluate, and adjust project activities based on performance.

## Project Budget

### Cost Breakdown

1. **Renewable Energy**: $5 million
2. **Energy Efficiency**: $3 million
3. **Afforestation and Reforestation**: $2 million
4. **Capacity Building and Training**: $1 million
5. **Monitoring and Evaluation**: $0.5 million

### Funding Sources

- **Green Climate Fund (GCF)**: $10 million
- **National Government**: $3 million
- **Private Sector Contributions**: $2 million

## Project Risks and Mitigation Strategies

### Potential Risks

1. **Technological Challenges**
   - Mitigated by engaging experienced technical experts.
2. **Financial Constraints**
   - Addressed through diversified funding sources.
3. **Social Acceptance**
   - Ensured through community engagement and participatory approaches.

## Project Evaluation

### Indicators

1. **Renewable Energy**: Percentage of energy generated from renewable sources.
2. **Energy Efficiency**: Reduction in energy consumption per unit output.
3. **Afforestation**: Number of trees planted and survival rates.

### Monitoring and Reporting

- Regular progress reports submitted to the GCF.
- Annual audits conducted by independent evaluators.
- Community feedback incorporated into project adjustments.

# As of April 2014

## NEMA would have the responsibility of project management, reporting and budgeting.

NEMA was established in 2008 as the principal government agency tasked to coordinate and oversee the implementation of the National Water Policy. NEMA has operational offices in all 47 counties in Kenya, and currently engages capacity of over 300 technical officers and 14 Regional Coordinators.

## The WRA is a state corporation under the Ministry of Water and Irrigation, tasked to regulate, manage and allocate the water resources in coordination with other relevant stakeholders. It is also mandated to ensure sustainable use of water for the benefit of all Kenyans.

## The MDF seeks the National Flood Forecasting and Early Warning Centre, which is currently under development by the Kenya Meteorological Department (KMD). This centre will be responsible for monitoring and forecasting flood-and-drought hazards, such as real-time hydrological data collection, early warning dissemination and risk management. The KMD has also developed a prototype for a national flood forecasting system.

## Engagement with civil society organizations and other relevant stakeholders

The Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources Plan describes the engagement with the private sector, development agencies, county governments and communities in water resources management and conservation.

# L6: Efficiency and effectiveness

## Financial sustainability

### For the current analysis, previous submissions of this FP considered a period of 10 years. The current submission considers a period of 30 years. This is because the financial model has been updated to reflect the current analysis. The overall submission presents an estimate analysis for a period of 30 years.

The project estimates that monetary benefits would be generated USD 25 billion over the life of the project, with an internal rate of return (IRR) of 18%. The net present value (NPV) is USD 7.9 billion and the benefit-cost ratio (BCR) is 3.0. The project is expected to generate sufficient revenue to cover both the capital cost and the operating costs over its life.

The total investment required for the project is estimated at USD 15 billion, with a loan-to-value ratio of 80%. The project will be financed through a combination of grants, loans and equity contributions. The project will also require ongoing support from development partners to ensure that it is implemented in a sustainable manner.

The overall objective of the project is to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of water resources management in Kenya. This will be achieved by improving the capacity of the government to manage water resources, increasing the availability of water for agricultural use, and enhancing the resilience of communities to climate change.
